### The Regional Measure — one pass

Sixteen fours: twelve by yield and four houses. Four boards. Every fighter's dram is combed
at the gate (Halloran; sixty-four thimbles; all read W full, I trace; nothing knocked).
Rhea Sorn signed Seven's entry as its attached officer: *T7, att., under my hand. Rowan:
recovery. Finch: reserve, a lamp. R.S.*

**Friday, the Great Floor.** An oval sixty paces long, four thousand seats in banked oak
tiers; a floor of oak blocks set on end over a brick undercroft that was once a cistern, with
piers four paces apart each way; a brass stud near the middle, between four piers; the
Circuit's mast on the rim above the south tier with twenty-four pens. Noa sits at it with a
white flag and a red; Mr. Adlam sits a step along with the Works' step glass. A still door
opened on this floor booms. The litter is "the Alderman."

*The Lane* (Circuit rules as before; rule nine for any change).

| Lane | Carry 1 | Carry 2 | Carry 3 | Final |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Seven v. Old Four (10 a.m.; Seven's line north) | 0–4: Old Four's box (Brask and Wace as posts, Edda's wall leaned across their shoulders, Gower inside with the litter); a push on one man comes out at the other. Wace downs Jude; litter home at 2:40. | 4–2: Brask calls "Give!" and Edda drops the wall; Perrin and Jude fall through and are downed. Kiva's stop pins Brask over a pier; Anwen leans on the thinned wall; Jude downs Brask from behind; Perrin walks the litter home at 3:10. | 4–1: no box. Brask twice stands where Kiva means to plant, and takes two halves off her (a down). Jude downs Gower; litter home with nine seconds left. | **Seven 8, Old Four 7** |
| Seven v. Halden (3 p.m.; Kiva declares Ember, one change) | 2–3: Kellan leaves Kiva alone and downs Jude. | 1–4: Kiva calls "changing on three" to put a stop in front of Pettit; **Kellan leaves the floor a quarter-count after her, dark, and lays the flat of his blade on her ribs at the top, in the gap.** Down. She calls *off* and comes down on her shoulder. White flag: "a crossing begun… not landed… not a stack." No incident. Pettit carries the litter home. | 3–1: still door only. Jude takes a half off Kellan's forearm. | **Halden 8, Seven 6** |

Kiva's Friday dram is not stated; by the tape's shape the unlanded crossing cost the bound and
the cliff. The hand was white nearly two hours and came back "to less."

Halden won the Lane (beat Twenty-Eight in the final by two; Selka Dray twice got a blade on
Kellan's forearm). *The Bench* (six banks of six cells, a hurry planted in one; find it,
quench that one, re-tune, restart): Twenty-Eight first (Wilf Marten, under three minutes);
Seven third, in a little over four minutes, Anwen leading with Perrin and Jude, each step said
aloud before it was done. Ysra Crest stood at the master stop. Kiva sat in the west tier with
Orla. After two boards Seven stood sixth of sixteen.

**Saturday, the Pit.** As before (seventy paces by forty; the Kiln north, the sump south, the
race down the middle), with three changes since the spring: the south link is in a green iron
box with a rod along the foot of the east wall to **a long handle beside the load board**; the
lattice house has **its own buried feeder**, so the race can be dropped and the lattice stays
lit; and **"the Row,"** twelve oak posts under the east rail, each with a small lit cell box.
Eight purse sheets lie in a row down the west half; the Graywater gate stands at the head of
the first, twelve paces west of the race.

*The Carry:* Old Four first by half a minute; Seven sixth (Perrin's first lift since the
spring, a dummy up the sump ladder on his shoulder). Aggregate, posted at noon: **first
company for the Call: Halden License, Old Four (att.), Team Twenty-Eight, T7 (att.)**, Seven
fourth by one mark over a Saltmere house four.

*The Call* (new; "what they've made of the Weighbridge"): a skinned string from the Kiln, the
top four rungs one to a team in board order, the bottom four Graywater's; a husk let out of
the chute after each rung, one to a team; the Row as a street; a casualty dummy for each team
to the west gate. Read off the foreman's slate in the five columns. Foreman: Hollis Vane.
Safety crew: Graywater (Rhea Sorn; Pike; four wards including Imrie and **Greta Stroud, back
on the line**; Ruddock; Nairn watching from a camp stool with a stick). Home Line closing crew
(Bettan). Keeper Dunmore at the load board; Warden Sedge in the lattice house.

| Time | Event |
|---|---|
| 2:00 | Lid up. String out, to the gate. |
| | Rung one, Halden: Kellan goes up dark, asks, comes down on "not yet," cuts for four-tenths; sealed at 39 (Yarrow). Husk one: Hesketh's lure, Pettit's kill. |
| | Rung two, Old Four: Gower on Wace's word; sealed at 44. Husk two: Edda's ward across its road; Brask carries the dummy. |
| | Rung three, Twenty-Eight: Selka Dray waits through one "not yet," cuts on "now"; sixty drams open on the third sheet. |
| about 2:19 | Sedge shouts that there are four against the gate and she sent for one. The chute gives husk three (Twenty-Eight's), husk four (Seven's, two minutes early) and **two that nobody ordered.** |
| 2:20 | The four do not turn to the Row. They trot south along the race, stop at the thirty-fifth pace, and stand at the corners of a square eight paces on a side, facing in. A white cord as thick as a wrist runs from each to the next at chest height. |
| | Marten lays his cloth two paces behind the south-east husk. Rhea shouts "Twenty-Eight! Stand!" Kiva shouts "Dray! Don't take the corner! It closes!" Dray hears, and goes on her catcher's word. A clean cut; more than half the ash on the canvas. |
| two counts later | **The cord comes off the dead corner, drops across Dray's left leg above the knee, and the other three haul it taut on her.** She goes dark at once and orders Marten to finish his count; he seals at 50. |
| 2:21 | **Vane strikes the Call.** Dunmore drops the race (forty rim lamps out) and quenches the Row. Lid ordered down. The first string stays parked on Graywater's gate. |
| | The wall comes down from the cord to the cinders and stands above it to twice a man's height. Inside, over the race, **a tear, outside the lattice.** Lowen Brask: "That's a hold." A string begins to rise in it, a knot to about twenty counts. |
| about 2:25 | The options are refused, each by the person who knows (section 5). Kiva: "It wants catching." Limits stated; Orla, Aske and Ysra answer from the rail, the gate and the rim; **Vane reads the direction with the cost first; "Do you take it?" "I'm frightened… Then yes."** |
| | Birch calls the Halden four off a struck floor. **Kellan: rule nineteen; "Renn. Seven. Kill."** Roles declared (section 5). |
| about 2:30 | **Kiva: "Hand, to Ember, to still. Rowan, changing on three, and on five."** On Brask's "in" she slides the ward under the cord; on "out" the haul throws her; fold, shut; gap; bound four paces south-east; shut at the top; gap; plant. **"Planted! Six!"** |
| | Perrin and Marten drag Dray ten paces along the ground, then Perrin carries her on his shoulder to the west gate (Perrin cutaway). Kiva holds the corner ten counts, counting in fives against the hold's fours. |
| | **"Back!"** on Brask's "in": Kiva gives the corner to the ground and shuts; Kellan (north-east), Jude (north-west) and Gower (south-west) cut their posts. The whole hold comes down "like a wet sheet let go from a line." Pike: "Dark! One." |
| | The new string (seven knots, the eighth coming) reads; it passes Kiva and goes to **the student gate at the head of the sixth sheet (Anwen in the middle, Edda, Hesketh, Twenty-Eight's ward).** The wall dips a hand at the south end and comes level. |
| | **Every lamp on the dead rim shows a point of light once.** Dunmore: "T-0." The mast shows a notch on all sixteen pens eleven seconds after her second cliff (told in chapter 40). |
| +60 counts | Nothing crosses the fallen ring. Then the closing crew goes in. |
| **3:10** | **"Closed!"** (Bettan). |
| to about 4:30 | Two strings are cut as a call: Rhea Sorn the old one (five rungs), Birch the new one's bottom four; the new string's top four are student rungs under Vane's slate (Old Four; Twenty-Eight with a reserve blade; Seven; Halden, lent by Birch). **Seven's rung, about 3:30: Kiva sets the mark and calls; Pru has the whole cord; Jude cuts for a full second on Kiva's "now"; sealed at 49.** |
| 5:00 | Vane reads the slate (section 6). |

## 2. Character state at exit

**Kiva Rowan.** Provisional Third Division, Crooked Line third team; **place, conditions and
sponsor unchanged**, by the Chair's own statement on the 19th.
- **Certificate** (Orla, the 24th of the seventh month, after the sixth counted crossing):
  *One crossing, Ember to still, low, to a mark. Declared to a count. On a sanctioned mast,
  read by the Chair's reader. One in seven days, the left hand seen by a healer the next
  morning. Not within three paces of open ash or any charged cell. Not certified: a stack of
  any kind. Any third door. Anything I have not seen six times.* Recovery only, dark hands,
  stands as before.
- **Chair's conditions added** (the 2nd of the seventh month): no second door on any Circuit
  floor save by a crossing declared to a count and read from a sanctioned mast by a reader of
  the office, at the time; a stack so read is an incident; not at the Bench; recovery role
  only on any floor with a contained rift. On the 19th of the ninth month the mast condition
  was made general, for any candidate with more than one door, at Selka Dray's request and
  under her name.
- **The six.** Complete: goes 2, 4, 5, 6, 7 and 8 (section 3). Orla: "It says it wasn't luck.
  That's the whole of it."
- **Three doors.** Order **hand, Ember, still** ("catch, carry, plant"). Five tries on the
  travelling mast (section 3): a jam, a clean hand-to-Ember crossing, an *off,* a stack she
  called herself, and **one clean, on the 29th of the eighth month.** One more in the field,
  under direction, on the 17th of the ninth. **Not certified, not classified, "not to be asked
  of her."** She has never done it twice in a week, never without a gap of days before, and
  both clean ones emptied the hand.
- What she now knows: a ward can be passed (its blow becomes the bound; she does not push
  off); a ward has a grip and must be folded shut, not let go; the second gap comes "out of a
  place already emptied"; a stack can arrive "dressed as thrift"; a floor has a behind, and it
  may be underneath; a hold cannot be broken by taking a corner; **across a gap the work comes
  with her and the beat does not** (felt once, in the first gap at the Pit).
- Skills and corrections gained: the fold (from Pru), with the first two left fingers held
  straight out of it, "passengers"; setting the stance by its corners; saying *one* on a
  landing before her reader; asking a team's question with the bad answer stated; **"Cord.
  Yours. All of it,"** said before she fumbles.
- **The pattern she is working on.** Answer withheld: to the Chair on the 2nd ("She's right"),
  at the kitchen table when Perrin voted no, to Anwen in the drying yard, to Dray on the
  cinders. Answer given, and caught: "I'd take the yard" to Kellan on the 25th.
- **Injuries.** Left knee: strapped on the 3rd of the seventh month; not mentioned as strapped
  afterward; no trouble on any landing. Heat-lines: the bottom thread hot once (the jam of the
  1st of the eighth month). **Left first and second fingers: permanent loss of fine sensation.**
  The road: white 58 counts (go 5), 75 (go 6), 95 (go 7), 130 (go 8, to the knuckle, blue at
  the nails), four minutes (hand to Ember, the 8th), ten minutes, twenty minutes and dull to
  the morning, then the whole hand gone from the wrist for forty hours after the clean
  three-door, returning "to less" (the first finger four breaths late; the second reporting
  the push and not the pin). Friday the 16th: one gap in a lane, white nearly two hours.
  Saturday the 17th, 7 a.m., Aske's card: *No go before the 23rd.* About 2:30 p.m.: two gaps.
  The hand went candle-white to two inches above the wrist. Wrist back Sunday evening; palm,
  thumb and last two fingers Monday morning. **Aske, Saturday the 24th:** the two fingers bend
  and grip and report pressure; they do not tell edge from flat, hot from cold, or one thread
  from two; "That's gone." Ward-trace ash is no use ("nothing in there for the ash to speak
  to"). The Order's infirmarian has written that a year's rest is for a hand that is hungry,
  not one that has been emptied.
- **The choice, as the page shows it:** declined the Order's year on the 4th of the seventh
  month ("I want the six and the third door more than I want the fingers sure. I may be
  wrong. Nobody told me to."); spent the week's one crossing in a lane on the 16th; on the
  17th stated the card and the two gaps herself, heard the price from her healer across the
  floor and from the foreman's slate, said that she was frightened, and said yes.
- **The disk.** Cold throughout: after each of the four crossings, after the clean three-door,
  at the Pit. She has written in the fourth column nine times in this movement (*Cold,* with
  two longer entries: the 1st and the 20th of the ninth month). She does not mind it.
- **The dream.** No recurrence. No fever on the night of the 17th; Mara's sheet has the date
  and "Nothing. No fever."
- Her book's last line: *Rowan, K. Catcher. Hand as before.*

**Mara Rowan.** Gave Vane tea "and not forgiveness" on the 1st; asked what the plate's meter
and shutdown were; has the watch's half sheet under the brass weight. Answered two of the
Order's six questions (whether Kiva had had the dream more than once; whether she was ill
each time: yes and yes); will reply to the second letter that the reader "keeps its door in
mind." Told Kiva of her own palm and gave no advice. **Passed the practical on the 7th of the
ninth month; Grade Two restored, the left her reading hand;** relit the hall panel, quenched
since the 13th of the fifth month. Sat in the Pit's east terrace on the 17th, said beforehand
that she would not come to the rail, stood, and did not come down. From the 19th she reads
the Ferren bank and the Brickfields trunk for Examiner Rusk.

**Orla Dane.** Not suspended. "You'll keep the stick. He'll keep the pen. Neither of you signs
without the other." Counted the six; added the certificate line; defined competition, stack
and sequence; would not put the three-door on any paper. Decided after the clean one to spend
no further week on it before the Regional ("a hand bought a yard or it bought a floor"). On
the 17th said from the rail what Kiva was passed for, what she had seen (once clean, three
misses), who could stop it, and "I don't know that it will work." Handed the Chair her own
account as sponsor, including the cost; Ysra countersigned "as the ward-holder who put her
name to his entry." **Wrote to Jessamy Hart in the seventh month, four lines, care of a
pump-fitter's yard on the south coast** (*There is another. She writes everything down. I did
not know to teach you that. O.D.*); unanswered; not written to be answered.

**Anwen Pryce.** Mace's line ended on the 21st of the seventh month. On her last night she
told a six-year man that his relieving wall was a hand short. Mace's card: *P., end of wall,
low arch, four weeks. No dip. Told a six-year man he was short;* "Come and see me when the
Regional's been read." She decided not to walk up the Rowan stair unasked. **Asked on the
22nd; answered on the 25th: she stands Seven's Hold on terms** (asked on the floor every
time, never taken as read; she calls the gate; "I'm not done… but it'll bear weight").
Abstained on the three-door ash until she had seen what a miss does to the hand; voted for it
on the 9th of the eighth month on condition that the *why* and the likely cost are written
and read out before each go, and read it on the line on the 29th. Led Seven's Bench (third).
At the Pit: called the step for a four-ward student gate that took a Flask's face for the
best part of twenty minutes; it dipped once, by a hand, and came up. **Mace has put her name
on a relief's place on the night van's ward line for next summer.** She told Kiva before her
own mother. **"I'm done minding… I've not forgotten. I looked, and those aren't the same."**

**Perrin Cade.** Ribs: top row of strapping off in the seventh month; passed to the shoulder
on the Wednesday before the Regional; "passed over my head" by the 25th, with the plate to
try on Thursday the 29th. Kept the tally when Kellan was at the refinery. **Voted no to the
three-door ash on the 25th of the seventh month** (it is Weighbridge Street's ash; Jude has
never cut on Seven's cloth), was asked what he would put in its place, and wrote the entry.
**Proposed the line himself a fortnight later, with his own brake** (two *offs* running end
it). Hung and released the sandbag. At the Pit: Carry; told Dray she was being rescued,
dragged and then carried her forty paces with his back to the hold; the ribs held.

**Kellan Renn.** Stated in the Chair's room, unasked, that he was entered under the Works'
seal and what the shed foreman said next. Cut Halden's top rung from the 3rd of the seventh
month (Pettit moved to Carry; Birch "decided on your feet"); taught the house four to move its
catcher in and to ask. Signed Seven's entries without voting on the three-door ration,
without a head's share of the Regional, and without being told the count. Told Kiva on the
14th that he would go for the gap and not for Perrin's ribs; did; learned afterward that it
was her week's go ("I'd have done exactly the same and felt sick first"). **On the 17th, when
Birch called the house four off a struck floor, he stood on rule nineteen and declared for
Seven;** cut the north-east post on Kiva's word; knelt on a corner of Seven's rung in a black
jacket with the cord torn off one cuff. The Works has released him "with its thanks" and paid
the stipend to the 17th. Birch has offered the yard for the winter; **he has not answered.**

**Noa Bexley.** Reader to the Chair's office for the candidate, with a white flag and a red.
Used the white twice (the unlanded crossing; the three-door). Reported no notch on the Great
Floor's twenty-three idle pens (the 10th of the seventh month); a hair on Kiva's one pen
eleven seconds after the yard three-door, "not entered as fact"; and **a notch on all sixteen
pens at the Pit eleven seconds after her second cliff.** His page now has a heading: *Not yet
weighed.*

**Jude Talley.** Seven's Kill for the Regional by name, on three conditions (his name on the
yield line; a head's share of that week's yield; the mark set for a blade that takes a full
second and comes down heavy). Has six drams credited by name. Cut the north-west post ("a
second and a tenth") and Seven's rung.

**Pru Finch.** Taught Kiva the fold. Reserve at the Regional. Had the whole cord on Seven's
rung, crossed it wrong at the third turn, and found it herself.

**Selka Dray.** Seated as Team Twenty-Eight's blade; second step on the river flats on the
27th of the sixth month (53 drams bagged at 48; Grade First, 104 counts), **lighting after
the cloth.** Her objection: "I don't want you off the floor any longer… I want you where I can
see the needle." At the Pit she cut inside her step, on her catcher's word, and was pinned;
went dark at once; made Marten finish the count; counted the hold's breath from under it and
Kiva's ten. **Left leg above the knee, the bone, and a burn of cold.** She has not changed her
mind and says so; has written to the Chair asking that the mast condition stand for anybody
with more than one door, "and that it worked"; "I can hold both. I've been practicing." Not a
friend.

**Lowen Brask.** Back from a summer on Home Line holds (the Ferren in the seventh month, the
flats in the eighth). Built a hold out of Old Four in a lane; told Kiva that her answer broke
his and not a real one. **Named the square, said why each remedy would fail, and called its
breath for the catch and for the drop.** Vane wants him on the Line's roll in the spring; he
has "thought."

**Rhea Sorn.** Helped the Circuit design the Call and distrusts how well it was mended. Kept
the first string on her gate and cut five rungs. Team Seven is still attached to her; the
board will want a Flask stood on her own van "before it takes the brackets off." Told Kiva
that in twenty-three years she has never named a price first. She burned the card the night
of the Weighbridge. No offer is made.

**Hollis Vane.** Read Kiva's entry to the watch; answered the recall motion from the first
leaf; undertook to come to Coper Street himself, in daylight, with seven initials, if a recall
ever carries. Foreman of the Call. **Second direction to Kiva in the ledger, with the cost
column restored and filled before:** *Direction to K. Rowan, unplaced. Three doors in
sequence, hand, Ember, still, declared. Cost, as stated before by her healer: the first two
fingers of the left hand, not to return. As stated by the candidate: two gaps on a hand that
had one yesterday. Reason: a blade of Twenty-Eight under a hold's hem, and no other hand on
this floor that can take it off her. I have watched her miss.* **And after:** *the first two
fingers of the left hand, without fine feeling. Not returned.*

**Ysra Crest.** Was a Wren House ward-holder on the Bench in 2660, held the wall behind which
the two burned students were dragged, **had signed Jessamy Hart's entry**, and sat on the
board that sealed his file. Could not tell a crossing from a stack by eye and wrote so on a
slate. Has opened a page headed *Witnessed* with one entry, and posted under the Regional's
result: *three doors opened in sequence… The candidate is not thereby classified. She is not
thereby certified. It is not to be asked of her. It is written here because it occurred.*

**Others.** **Greta Stroud**: back on the ward line; warned the floor off laying a ward on
the cord; did up Kiva's cuff ("Let them"). **Pike**: "It isn't in your hand. It's that word."
**Healer Aske**: named the price at seven and at half past two. **Mr. Adlam**: pleasant and
lawful throughout; agreed in the Chair's room that the Works' own caution supports the
catcher; wrote to the Chair under his own name about the sixteen pens and asks to be second
when Noa prints. **Foreman Birch**: called his students off by his paper, let Hesketh stand a
gate, cut as a chartered blade, lent his four for a rung. **Gower**: cut a post on a word.
**Mrs. Cobb**: nine mornings at 2C's cupboard; a receipt for Seven's dram.

## 3. The goes

**Ember to still, on a sanctioned mast.** Each 0.31 (0.02 + 0.26 + 0.03).

| Go | Date | Where | Changed problem | White | Count | Glass |
|---|---|---|---|---:|---|---:|
| 5 | Mon 2nd, 7th month | Old Yard | The Chair watching; two slates before the tape (Orla *Crossing;* Ysra *I cannot tell*) | 58 | Three | 0.94 |
| — | Mon 9th | Great Floor | The step booms on a hollow floor: **no go** (rule one) | — | — | 0.94 |
| 6 | Tue 10th | Great Floor | Stance set by its four corners, onto the piers | 75 | Four | 0.63 |
| 7 | Tue 17th | Old Yard | None; Kellan absent | 95 | Five | 0.32 |
| 8 | Tue 24th | Old Yard | One go and a hundredth in the glass; the stack offers itself as thrift; she waits a hair longer than ever, the gap is nearly two-fifths of a second, and she lands hard | 130 | **Six** | 0.01 |

The hundredth was carried into the entry of the 25th. Sanctioned record now: sixteen misses
before the six as in Movement Four; successes: the Pit, and six on a mast.

**Three doors, on the travelling mast.** Price whole: 0.02 (ward, one tap) + 0.26 + 0.02
(bound) + 0.26 + 0.03 (stance) = **0.59.** Written on Kiva's page of the 25th: order; where
each limb is; the price; who may stop it (Orla; Perrin's *off*; Kiva herself, after the ward
and at the top; the thread before the line); how it ends early (ward splashes: stand still;
Ember not clean: *off* and roll; thread warm in the air: land, say so, a miss; any white
before: no go). Yard form: Perrin's sandbag "Gerald," a hundredweight on the shed hoist,
swings through the scraped line from the left and behind; ward curved at the left shoulder;
fold from the far edge to the palms; one low bound to the cross; stance.

| Try | Date (8th month) | What happened | Tape | Paid from | Hand |
|---|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Hand to Ember, 1 | Tue 1st | **Jam.** The two dull fingers did not report a rim of ward; bottom thread hot; she called *one* and *off.* | 0.11 (0.02 + 0.09) | her ration | pink |
| Hand to Ember, 2 | Tue 8th | **A crossing.** The struts "caught" the bag's shove; her knees were straight. Landed on the Ember. | 0.30 (0.02 + 0.26 + 0.02) | her ration | white four minutes |
| Whole, 1 | Tue 15th | First gap clean; she listened in the air; a strut clung; ***off.*** | 0.31 | team | ten minutes |
| Whole, 2 | Tue 22nd | First gap clean; **the second transition stacked;** soft landing; she said "One" with the stance still open, before Noa found it. | 0.39 (0.02 + 0.26 + 0.02 + 0.06 + 0.03) | team | twenty minutes; dull till morning |
| Whole, 3 | Tue 29th | **Clean.** "Changing on three, and on five." Stance held from six to thirteen; Orla felt the bag's swing arrive in the ground under her palm. | 0.59 | team | gone from the wrist, forty hours |

Aske's ruling of the 9th: a single gap, one a week as before; two gaps in one go, nothing for
a fortnight after; no white on the day. No go was made on the 6th or the 13th of the ninth
month. His card for the Regional week: *One, in the week, if the lane wants it. Then nothing.*

**In the field:** the unlanded crossing of the 16th; the three-door of the 17th (two cliffs
of 0.26 a little under two seconds apart, two hundredths between; from her field dram, of
which she had spent nothing).

## 4. Records and rulings

**The watch, Sunday the 1st of the seventh month** (written in the back of the bearer's book):
*Heard. Not recalled. The plate is held of the Line and borne by K. Rowan. It is not
equipment. It is not an instrument, and it is not evidence. No hand but the bearer's is to be
laid on it, to test it or weigh it or show it, without a vote of the seven. / The bearer will
write in the fourth column within the week after any crossing, before a watch officer,
whatever she has to put.* The two votes for recall are written. In that room officers go by
their watches: the Ferren (she; seventy; thirty-four years in the chair), the River Flats (he;
moved the recall), the Lantern (she; voted for it), the Weir, the Sag (Vane); two are not
named. The Crooked Line's bursar asked whether the plate was the one in the ninth edition and
whether he might borrow it for the anniversary case; answered from the fourth edition, word
for word.

**The unread fourth-column entry** (traced by leave of all seven; read by the Reader; read to
the watch, then to Kiva on the 1st of the ninth month): *Bly, Thirza. Received 2466.* Her
*why:* she was a levee catcher and the bearer before her liked how she kept a count. Her
fourth column, in the night hand: ***Warm, once, after the first crossing. I have crossed
nine times since. Cold. I put this in the night hand, that it may be kept and not made much
of. T.B.*** She does not say from which door to which, or how many doors she had. She carried
it thirty-five years. Kiva's entry beneath her own: *Cold. After three doors in sequence,
once, on a sanctioned mast. Cold.* And on the 20th: *Cold… After three doors in sequence,
under direction, with a hold's hem carried and planted for ten counts. Cold before, during
and after. / I did not ask it anything. It did not do anything.*

**The Chair on the bag, Tuesday the 3rd** (she states first that she is not a court and
cannot construe a patent): (1) the bag stays in the Wren House cold room under three seals,
the Works', the wardens' and the office's, opened with all three present or not at all; (2)
it is entered as made in Weighbridge Street on the 24th, off a Graywater call, catchers Pike
and K. Rowan; (3) the casualty reserve advances the street **56.5 drams of plain stock** that
day, to be repaid by whoever comes to own the bag; (4) one dram is drawn for the reference
shelf and one for the Halden technical office (**111 remain**); (5) the Works' claim is
entered and not ruled on; it will go to the Compact's court; (6) **a catcher of any step may
tag a bag red on grounds stated; the bag is combed before it is panned; the tag goes on the
slate under the catcher's name; "A catcher's word is sufficient to withhold. It is not
sufficient to prove. It may stop a bag. It may not name one";** (7) until the court has
spoken no ash that speaks on the seventh reed above a trace is issued, bought, burned or
settled on any Circuit floor, and every Regional dram is combed at the gate. On "what paper":
"a catcher's tag, a chartered officer's slate, a comb, and a caution printed by the Halden
Works in 2391."

**The Chair on the stack:** no crossing counts toward anything unless the tape shows the
cliff; the sponsor's eye is not sufficient; Noa is reader to the office; one of the three
remaining goes to be made on the Great Floor.

**The Order's second letter** (unsigned; "the Order did not sign"): a year as a guest of the
house under its rule, silence at table and one door, every other kept shut, nothing asked and
nothing taught unasked; a sister knew the hand from how it lay ("Our old wall-holders carry
theirs so. Such a hand is not burned. It is spent, and it fills with a long rest… Some of
ours have come back whole in a year. Not all.").

**Vane's ledger**, *Directions to the Unplaced:* now three entries (E.K., 2471; Vane, the
30th of the fifth month; and the direction of the 17th of the ninth, with the cost before and
after).

**The Chair's page,** headed *Witnessed:* ruled for what was done, where, on whose direction,
what it cost, and who read the tape. One entry. Filed beside it: Adlam's four lines under his
own name, and Noa's.

## 5. The hold (shown, not explained)

What the page shows, in order:
1. Four skinned husks, two of them unordered, ignored twelve lit boxes and stood at the
   corners of a square over the race, facing in.
2. Their shine ran outward and met as a white cord at chest height from each to the next; a
   frost wall hung from it to the cinders and stood above it.
3. A tear opened inside the square, over the race, outside the Kiln's lattice, and a string
   began to come up through it.
4. Cut at one corner, the cord came off the dead post, dropped, and was hauled taut by the
   other three, in time: four counts hauling, four easing.
5. The Ferren pump bank, across the road, went into step "at the instant that square stood
   up" (Adlam's glass) and **did not come out when the race was dropped.**
6. Dropping the race did not make the hold let go.
7. Refused, each by its owner: dragging Dray out (the cord "goes straight… through the tear,
   and through whoever's got her by the arms": Kiva; Brask); cutting the cord (both ends fly:
   Brask; it would turn a blade: Rhea); a ward under it (it takes the ward: Stroud); a
   still-door's lift (hands go into it: Brask); cutting the three posts (the whole hem comes
   down on her: Brask).
8. A ward put under the cord for one tap, on the slack, and shut before the haul could take
   hold, was thrown; the haul came across the gap **and its count did not.** Planted in the
   ground four paces off, it held the corner for ten counts while the hold went on hauling.
9. Released on the slack at the same word as three cuts, the hold came down whole and soft
   and lay in a ring. It was left sixty counts. It "was not ash, and nobody in Meridian had a
   rule for it." It was swept under the Chair's seal.
10. Unwalled, the string read, passed the nearest worked thing that was dark, and went to the
    nearest lit gate.
11. Every lamp on the dead rim showed a point of light once; sixteen pens notched eleven
    seconds after Kiva's second cliff.
12. Every rung of the string that came through the held tear, and Seven's bag with them,
    keeps time "loud" and knocks on the comb. The Kiln's own string read a trace, as all this
    year's ash has.

Brask: "They're doing what we do. I don't know who showed them. I'm not saying anybody did."
The Home Line has posted, without a reason, that no tear is to be held for a licensing until
further word. **Nobody on the page says why.** No character connects the hold, the stepped
ash, the bank, the lamps, the notch, the disk and the dream. The Ferren asks aloud whether
the watch has been asking the plate the wrong question ("I've never thought to ask what else
was happening when it did") and calls it a notion.

## 6. Ledgers

**Team Seven's book.**

| Date | Entry | Sealed medical (W) | Practice glass | Held | Notes |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Sat 30th, 6th | Fourth signature (Anwen); 8.4 may be entered | 2.0 | 1.25 | 1.6 | Credited 8.4 |
| 2nd–24th, 7th | Four crossings at 0.31 | 2.0 | 0.01 | 1.6 | 0.94, 0.63, 0.32, 0.01 |
| **Wed 25th, 7th** | **Entry signed by four** | 2.0 | 0 | 4.91 | In hand 8.4 + 1.6 + 0.01 = 10.01. Kennel floors, three: 2.1. Regional reserve, half a dram a fighter: 2.0. Returned at par to the wardens' account, Weighbridge Street, for the Pellow's boxes: 1.0. Held 4.91. *Talley, J., entered as Kill… with a head's share of that week's yield. Renn, K., takes none. Three-door, K. Rowan: not carried.* |
| Wed 9th, 8th | Three-door line, Perrin's proposal | 2.0 | — | 3.11 | 1.8 from held: three whole goes "and the misses out of the same"; Perrin keeps the word; the *why* and likely cost written and read before each. |
| 15th, 22nd, 29th | 0.31, 0.39, 0.59 | 2.0 | — | 3.11 | 1.29 spent of the 1.8. |
| **Sun 25th, 9th** | **Entry signed by four** | 2.0 | — | **5.62** | Regional reserve not drawn, returned (2.0); 0.51 of the three-door line not spent, returned. *Credited from the Pit, the 17th, by the foreman's pot: 24.0. Clean; W, full; I, a trace. To Talley, J., Kill, by name, a head's share: 6.0. To the book: 18.0.* |

Check: 10.01 = 2.1 + 1.0 + 1.29 + 5.62. The sealed W reserve was never opened; Anwen had it
written that it is not training ash, and Aske has said that it is no use for the fingers.
Mrs. Cobb's receipt: *Recd. of T7, one dram, plain, combed. For the boxes. E. Cobb, Warden.*
Kiva's two hand-to-Ember tries (0.11, 0.30) came off her own weekly half dram. Pru is paid
off the Circuit's reserve card.

**The Pit, the 17th: Vane's slate.** *The Call, opened at two. A tear on the open floor,
outside the lattice, held by four husks, at twenty past. Struck, twenty-one past. Closed, ten
past three, by the Home Line. Lives: none lost. Hurt: Dray, blade, the left leg above the
knee, the bone, and a burn of cold. Rowan, catcher, the left hand; see the direction. Works:
the race dropped an hour and ten minutes by the keeper. The Row quenched, twelve boxes, by
the keeper; none fouled. The Ferren bank in step since twenty past two and not come out; that
is the Works' to enter. Tinned, clean: five hundred and twelve drams. Put by, red, on
catchers' words and a comb: five hundred and three and seven-tenths. And a ring of something
that is not in the yield book, swept under the Chair's seal. Remarks: one. A blade cut a husk
inside her step, on her catcher's word, before the foreman had read the floor. The foreman
had not called a stand. The remark is the event's.*

**The pot.** A struck floor is shared the Line's way, everything clean in one: civic half
256; casualty reserve 64; schools 96; teams 96, **24 to each of the four teams**, whoever
tinned it. Halden tinned most and draws the same as Seven, whose own rung is red-tagged. The
Works did not object. The red row goes to the cold room under three seals with each catcher's
name. Seven's bag: *Red. Not for the ground. Stepped. On a catcher's word and a comb. K.
Rowan,* in her own hand, under the sixth ruling. No per-team yield and no new yield standing
is on the page.

**Result, posted Monday the 19th:** *The Regional Measure, 2680. Read on three boards; the
Call struck. 1. Halden License. 2. Old Four (att.). 3. Team Twenty-Eight. 4. T7 (att.).*

## 7. New people and places

People (pronouns as written; author's choice):
- **Thirza Bly** (she; bearer from 2466; a levee catcher; a name in the book).
- **Wilf Marten** (he; Team Twenty-Eight's catcher, the thin Wren House boy of chapters 24
  and 32, now named).
- Halden license four: **Pettit** (he; second-year, fair, heavy in the leg; moved from Kill
  to Carry), **Hesketh** (she; Hold, tall), **Yarrow** (he; Catch, a still-door), and
  **Foreman Birch** (he; chartered; square; blunt).
- Old Four's catcher, **Wace** (he; a still-door).
- Graywater ward line: **Tibb** (he; nine years; hums) and **Stiles** (he; six years; a
  shoulder wrong since the weir).
- Kellan's mother appears (still unnamed; twenty-two years a grader; the reference jars).
- Unnamed: the watch officers; the Chair's clerk; a gate steward; the Circuit's bursar (by
  letter); Twenty-Eight's ward and its reserve blade; a Saltmere house four.

Places and things: the Chair's hearing room; the Halden refinery gate (a hand with a flame in
the palm; 612 rivets), its grading room at the top of the old still house, and its license
yard (blue brick, a lattice house, a paid ward line, a contained six-knot string); **the
Great Floor** and its undercroft; the Pit's boxed link, long handle, buried feeder and Row;
the step glass (the Works' instrument for finding the cell in a bank that has fallen out of
step; "like a clock with no hands"); "the Alderman"; the Call; the Chair's *Witnessed* page.

All new names were searched for in this book's manuscript and provenance, the other books'
manuscripts and registries, and `universe/` before use. Two first choices were dropped for
matching existing names: "Ivo" (book two) became Wilf, and "Orme" (the prologue) became
Stiles.

## 8. Plants and open threads carried forward

| Plant | Status at exit |

| Six witnessed repeats | **Done.** A certificate line for one Ember-to-still crossing. Not mastery. |
| The crossing's cost | Still a cliff of 0.26 with nothing open. Unexplained. Now also known: across a gap the work comes and a stepped beat does not. Felt once. Not measured. |
| Three-door configuration | Done twice, clean (a yard; the Pit). **Publicly witnessed, entered by the Chair, uncertified, unclassified, not to be asked for.** Never repeated inside a week. |
| Two-finger sensation | **Paid. Permanent.** |
| Disk | Cold. Held of the Line; not equipment, not evidence; recall lost two to five. An earlier bearer wrote the same thing: warm once, after the first crossing, then cold through nine more. What it is for: unknown. The Ferren's question stands as a question. |
| Dream | No recurrence. Mara's page still sealed. |
| The Order | A door "on the latch." Its infirmarian has corresponded with Aske. Four of six questions unanswered. |
| T-0 and the notch | A second dead-ring T-0 at the Pit and a second sixteen-pen notch, both at eleven seconds after a first of its kind in public; nothing on the Great Floor's idle pens after a fourth crossing; a doubtful hair on one pen in the yard. Two witnesses now, one of them Halden's, in writing. Unexplained. |
| Stepped ash | The first bag: 111 drams under three seals, claim gone to the Compact's court. Now also 503.7 drams red-tagged from the Pit, and a ring that is not ash. A general red-tag rule exists. |
| **The Ferren pump bank** | In step since 2:20 on the 17th and not out, nine days on. The pumps have not dropped a stroke. Nobody knows how to take a bank out of step; the patent has no page for it. Quenching and refilling is four hours without pumps. |
| **The Brickfields trunk** | Falling into step a drum every two days or so: nineteen (Wed 21st), twenty (Fri 23rd), twenty-one (Sun 25th), with the links open the whole length. Nine drums remain; the last is **the Crooked Line School's core house.** Mara: "Eighteen days. If it keeps its time." |
| Bells | Mast five, the weigh-house and (Mara thinks) the weir, together, at four on the 26th. |
| Held tears | Suspended by the Home Line, no reason given. |
| Team Seven | Fourth at the Regional; attached still; a Flask on Rhea's van is wanted. Kellan undecided about the Halden yard. Jude has a yield line and six drams. Perrin to the plate, and his Hall's second team. Anwen has a relief's place for next summer. |
| Anwen and Kiva | Reformed by asking: "done minding," not forgotten. |
| Selka Dray | Hurt; unpersuaded; her rule now general. |
| Jessamy Hart | Written to. No answer. |
| Orla and Ysra | Both names on the sponsor's account of the hand. |
| Mara's license | Restored. |
| Lowen Brask | Wanted on the Line's roll in the spring. |
| Rhea Sorn | No offer made. |

## 9. Items for owner or editorial attention

Routes the author chose where the packet left room, and places where the draft may have gone
further than intended:

1. **The months are numbered** (fifth to ninth). Earlier chapters only say "the ninth month"
   for Mara's re-sit. Review should check that nothing in chapters 1–32 contradicts spring as
   the fifth month.
2. **Ysra Crest's history is new:** a ward-holder on the 2660 Bench who signed Hart's entry
   and sat on the board that sealed his file, and who did not speak to Orla for ten years. It
   gives her ruling on Orla weight; it also changes how chapters 1 and 8 read.
3. **An earlier bearer crossed.** Thirza Bly's line says the plate was warm once, after her
   first crossing, and cold through nine more. It makes Kiva's experience a pattern of two
   and implies a multi-door bearer in the 2460s. It gives no cause and no use. If the owner
   wants the disk's earlier record left blank, this is the line to cut; the watch could as
   easily refuse leave.
4. **The stronger variety is "a hold":** four husks standing a square with a tear inside it,
   echoing the Home Line's own held tears. It follows the skin (a ward's echo) and the Step
   (keeping time together) and adds a tear outside any lattice. Brask names it and refuses to
   say that anybody showed them. This is the movement's largest invention and it leans on a
   new detail of Home Line practice (a line leaned from holder to holder; "the weight goes
   round").
5. **What the three doors did** is a catch: a ward under a loaded cord for one tap, the throw
   taken as a bound, the haul planted and held as a corner for ten counts, then released on
   the same word as three cuts. The new claim is that the work crosses a gap and a stepped
   count does not. It is felt, once, by Kiva, and measured by nobody. It points at Movement
   Six and should be checked against what that movement is meant to keep.
6. **The permanent loss follows three choices on the page:** the Order's year declined; the
   week's crossing spent in a lane, where Kellan fairly used the gap; and two gaps the next
   day against a healer's card, with the price read out first. Aske and the Order's
   infirmarian agree afterward that rest would not now help.
7. **The Order made a kind offer** and is shown as right about hands. This is a larger and
   gentler role than the packet's "pressures."
8. **The hearing rulings** are the author's: three seals; 56.5 advanced; a dram each to the
   shelf and the Halden office; the general red-tag rule; no ash above I-trace on any Circuit
   floor; the patent claim sent to "the Compact's court," which has not been on the page
   before.
9. **The Regional's shape** is the author's: sixteen fours, four boards over two days, the
   Great Floor, the Call, entry of attached teams under their officer's hand, and a struck
   floor shared in one pot. The Call is struck, so the Regional is read on three boards and
   Seven is fourth.
10. **Seven's lane answer misleads a rival.** "Take a corner," called aloud by Kiva on
    Friday, is in Dray's mind on Saturday. The page has Dray say that she was listening to her
    catcher, not to Kiva, and Vane puts the remark on the event. Review should check that
    neither Dray nor Vane reads as careless.
11. **Kellan declares for Seven on a struck floor** by rule nineteen. Halden releases him
    civilly. Whether he returns to its yard is left open.
12. **A second T-0 and a second notch, in public.** Dunmore says of the lamps that it was "on
    this floor, with her on it." That is the nearest any character comes to a connection.
13. **The pump bank and the trunk** are the hook into Movement Six. "Eighteen days" to the
    school's core house is a clock the next movement inherits; it may be too tidy.
14. **Rhea Sorn makes no offer** and the brackets stay on. Team Seven's "owed Flask" was not
    stood in this movement.
15. **Mara passes her re-sit** (a paragraph) and keeps her word not to come to the rail.
16. **Orla wrote to Jessamy Hart.** Four lines; unanswered.
17. **Movement length is 45,623 words** (`wc -w`), at the foot of the working range. Chapters
    run 4,959 to 6,699. Forty-six sections, about 990 words a section. **Secondary viewpoint
    is about 13.7%** by plain section word count (Vane about 1,370; Anwen 1,199; Rhea 1,102;
    Kellan 986; Perrin 836; Orla 758; all established lenses); Kiva holds about 86.3%.
18. **No formula target has been measured** for these chapters. The first three boards of the
    Regional are compressed: one lane round, the Bench and the Carry are short on purpose.
19. Small facts taken from the checkpoints and not re-read in the manuscript should be
    checked: that Kiva writes with her right hand; the Great Floor has never been described
    before; Kellan's count of rivets; Jude's "feet in love with themselves"; Perrin's "strongest
    first-year his Hall had turned out in thirty years"; that the Kindling's deferral was
    written by a clerk of the Chair's office.
20. Arithmetic worth a mechanical check: Kiva "had made it seven times on a mast" (chapter
    37); "eight times" to the hall by the 1st of the ninth month; Aske's column of six figures;
    "seventeen days" from the 29th of the eighth month to the Regional; "nine days" and "nine
    more lines" at the pump house; Rhea's "five rungs"; "eleven weeks" in the Halden yard.
21. Pronouns chosen by the author: Bly, Hesketh and the Ferren she; Marten, Pettit, Yarrow,
    Birch, Wace, Tibb and Stiles he.

## 10. Deliberately not done

The disk does not warm, teach, speak, map, choose, open a door or answer; it is cold through
every go and through the Pit, and an earlier bearer's line says to expect no more. The dream
does not recur and supplies no tactic; the name Unroofed Sky is not on the page. The cause of
the Fall, the filter, the Makers and the disk's origin are not explained, and nobody says why
a tear throws stepped ash or why husks stood a hold. The three-door configuration is done
once in public, for about twelve counts, under a direction, and is not repeated, certified,
ranked or explained; no rank or bench figure is re-measured. The fingers are not cured by ash,
rank, rest or the result. No free practice ash appears: every crossing is 0.31 from the glass,
the pieces come from Kiva's ration, and the three-door line is voted, limited and partly
returned. The sealed medical reserve is not opened. Kiva is never seated outside recovery in a
sanctioned event; the three-door is under a recorded field direction after the strike. Kellan
keeps his Halden entry until the floor is struck. Anwen is on Mace's line until the 21st of
the seventh month. Selka Dray is not made a friend, and neither Halden, the Works, the Order
nor the watch is made a villain. The students close no tear and cut no bottom rung. No
secondary viewpoint is new. No review, scoring, repair, commit, push, publication or
narration was done by the author, and Movement Six has not been started.

## 11. What Movement Six opens on

State on the evening of Monday the 26th of the ninth month:

- **The grid.** A municipal pump bank of twenty-four has been in step for nine days and
  cannot be taken out; the step is walking the Brickfields trunk toward the collegiate
  quarter at about a drum every two days, with nine to go before the Crooked Line's core
  house. The Works can quench and refill, at four hours without pumps in a wet autumn. Three
  bells rang together at four.
- **The rifts.** A tear opened on an arena floor outside a lattice and was held by its own
  spawn. The Home Line has stopped holding tears. More than five hundred drams of loud
  stepped ash and a ring of something unnamed are under seal.
- **The record.** The Chair has written that three doors in sequence occurred and has refused
  to say what the girl is. A watch has refused to recall a plate. A Halden officer has put his
  own name to sixteen pens. A patent claim is before a court.
- **The people.** Kiva has eight fingers that report, a catcher's grip that depends on a word
  said to somebody else, one certified crossing a week, and a configuration that empties her
  hand. Anwen has a professional place for next summer. Kellan has an open offer. Perrin can
  lift. Selka Dray is in a board until the winter. Mara is licensed and is the one reading the
  bank. Seven is fourth, attached, with 5.62 held, 18.0 credited and 2.0 sealed.
- Withheld for Movement Six and after, intact: any explanation of the hold, the Step, the
  notch or the T-0s; what the disk is for; the crooked post's reason; a professional offer;
  Kiva's classification; whether what she felt in the gap can be used on a bank.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — books/book-2680-opening/STATE_LEDGER.md

# Kindling 2680 opening state ledger

Status: opening locks resolved 2026-10-08. Ready for the prologue and first
eight-chapter continuous prose run.

## Prologue and opening day

- Year 2680, during the six-hundredth-anniversary season of the Fall.
- The prologue's final historical snapshot is 2676: **Kiva Rowan**, thirteen,
  reaches the Kindling uninjured and opens all three known doors.
- Chapter One begins four years later. Kiva is seventeen and seeking collegiate
  placement for the 2680 season.
- Kiva lives with her aunt and legal guardian, **Mara Rowan**, in Meridian. Mara
  repairs municipal instruments and reconciles their paper records.
- The Satori disk reached Kiva through a sealed Home Line appointment by its prior
  bearer, not through blood descent. Kiva knows it is old and entrusted; she does
  not know its full public history or origin.
- Meridian's schools expect one first door and have procedures for unusual strength,
  but no accepted process for three simultaneous traditions.

## Knowledge boundaries

- General public: knows the Fall, Kindling, ranks, schools, Riftspawn, and ordinary
  filter language as history/science appropriate to the final world-state choice.
- Students: know institutional versions of Home Line history, not necessarily the
  surviving founder records.
- Orla Dane: knows the disk family and at least one suppressed prior anomaly.
- Kiva: does not know the disk's true origin, the full filter theory, or why
  all three doors opened.
- Kiva remembers the 2676 fever dream in sensory fragments: Glory-level figures
  converging, the sky/filter absent, pure presence pressing into the world, and every
  ash-lit thing responding. She does not know the later name **Unroofed Sky**, whether
  the dream predicts anything, or that older Glory accounts overlap it.
- Orla knows there are disputed historical fever accounts but does not begin Book One
  with proof that Kiva's dream matches them.
- Reader: sees evidence before explanation; no prologue solves the mystery.

## Physical and progression state

- All three doors open at Kindling, but none is mastered.
- The event leaves three fine, asymmetrical heat-lines from Kiva's sternum toward
  her left shoulder. They remain visible and become tender when the doors compete.
- The disk warms once, only after Kiva completes the doors in a correct deliberate
  sequence. It never instructs her or grants technique.
- The disk can amplify or carry the Unroofed Sky fever dream, but it cannot trigger
  on demand, answer questions, or supply a combat/engineering solution.
- Initial classification is incomplete or contradictory, not a secret top rank.

## Social state

- Kiva has a real home/family obligation in Mara's repair-and-records work.
- Anwen Pryce, Perrin Cade, and Kellan Renn have independent placements and
  goals before meeting the protagonist.
- Institutions have defensible reasons to fear a category they cannot safely test.

## Institutional and world locks

- Campus compact: **the Meridian Compact**.
- Mixed-path college: **the Crooked Line School**.
- Resilience/body college: **Stonehand Hall**.
- Medical, Ward, Sustain, and ash-engineering college: **Wren House**.
- Collegiate competition: **the Meridian Collegiate Circuit**; final regional
  event: **the Regional Measure**. Events include individual Measures, team lanes,
  ashcraft trials, rescue/extraction problems, and controlled Riftspawn engagements.
- The collegiate curriculum includes a staged field license: contained spawn anatomy
  and kill drills, supervised Handful kills, live-rift recovery, then higher-yield team
  responses. Book One devotes several connected chapters to Kiva's team learning how
  to kill Riftspawn and recover the ash cleanly before the first major live response.
- Above college are **chartered rift companies**: salaried professional teams that
  fight Riftspawn, close or contain rifts, rescue civilians, protect infrastructure,
  and recover ash. Standings reward closure, lives saved, protected infrastructure,
  clean ash, and rule compliance—not kills alone.
- Book One is regional. Book Two expands toward national competition and company
  politics. Book Three becomes international as the filter and ash grid destabilize.
- Mature hardened regional technology and scheduled wormhole stations exist. Travel
  is expensive and bounded; interplanetary normality does not.
- Classification uses physical instruments, projected tables, and human rulings.
  There is no private retinal or software-like status overlay.
- **Draw** is ash spent on standard work divided by that task's book/bench expectation:
  `1.0` is standard and lower is more efficient. A contested four-minute hold uses a
  book expectation of twelve hundredths of a dram.

## Ash-electric civilization and Glory

- Refined ash is the dominant finite energy source. **Ash induction** converts a
  controlled ash reaction into ordinary current for lights, pumps, heat,
  communications, medicine, workshops, transit, arenas, and rift sensing.
- Ash does not create free energy. Cells are graded, metered, consumed, reconciled,
  and vulnerable to contamination or runaway.
- **Ashcraft** blends magic and ash: a trained soul supplies pattern, intention,
  and control; ownerless ash supplies most of the fuel. Different traditions shape,
  contain, conduct, or sustain the reaction differently.
- Modern cells continue running after a Kindled operator starts and tunes them, but
  still require maintenance, replacement ash, and emergency shutdown.
- At **Glory**, a rare master can impress stable patterns at civic or industrial
  scale. Glory does not make ash infinite; it expands safe throughput, duration,
  precision, and the complexity of blended systems.
- That capability produces ash-power industrial dynasties as well as school orders
  whose masters pursue near-monastic devotion to one tradition. Some orders reject
  corporate ownership; some industry houses sponsor them; some Glory masters move
  between both worlds.
- Pro rift companies feed the ash grid. This creates prestige and public service,
  but also incentives to overhunt, hide risk, manipulate ash grades, or protect
  wealthy districts first.
- A sanctioned kill produces a recorded yield. Civic power, casualty reserve, school,
  and team shares are removed in that order. Student shares fund training cells,
  metered combat techniques, equipment, travel, and medical reserve; they are not
  unmetered personal treasure.
- Ash is recorded by **yield** (Handful, Flask, Barrel, Well), **grade** (purity and
  stability), and **resonance** (which patterns can use that spawn expression safely).
  A large dirty yield may be worth less than a small stable one.
- Killing does not grant rank automatically. Real combat can deepen capacity because
  the practitioner works at the edge of owned control and must later reproduce the
  lesson. Ash funds the next threshold; training makes the threshold survivable.
- Filter thinning increases both human potential and Riftspawn pressure. The opening
  book presents this first as unusually capable students and an unusually varied rift
  season. Later books prove the common Glory-pressure cause.

## First-season lasting cost

During the high-stakes regional sequence Kiva will permanently lose fine sensation
in the first two fingers of her left hand. Healing closes the injury but does not
restore exact touch. The cost is planted before it occurs and changes how she grips,
trains, and asks for help. Rank cannot erase it.

## Movement Three carried state (chapters 17–24)

Recorded 2026-10-11 after the consolidated same-author repair. **Passed by a separate
same-model informed targeted recheck; no second prose repair is justified.** The manuscript governs; full detail is in
`provenance/MOVEMENT-003-CONTINUITY.md`.

- **Calendar.** The month is still unnamed and has thirty days. The fifth company's Flask
  qualifier at the Pit is **Wednesday the 29th** (the first draft's "26th" was wrong by
  its own weekdays). Grading Thursday the 30th; board findings and standings **Friday the
  1st**; the investment vote **Saturday the 2nd**; the Old Yard **Sunday the 3rd**.
  Movement Four opens after Sunday the 3rd.
- **The sequence.** Kiva has completed one correct deliberate two-door sequence, Stride
  into stance: about nine seconds of stance, 0.31 of a dram (0.02 + 0.26 + 0.03), Draw
  near twelve. It was done under a recorded Home Line field direction, after the event
  was struck, outside her certificate. It is not certified. Orla's standard is "Show me
  six"; Team Seven has funded six sanctioned tries on three written conditions (a
  sanctioned mast every go; Orla on the crate; Noa reads after and never during).
- **The disk** warmed once, on the 29th. Kiva **first felt it after** she had shut the
  still door; nobody knows when it began. It was cold by the keeper's gate and has been
  cold since. It did not teach, speak, answer, or act.
- **Body.** Stood down until the **13th**: no lanes, no floors, nothing lit, no landings
  (Healer Aske's fortnight; the infirmary healer's ban is extended to match). The two
  left fingers were eased inside the hour with ward-trace ash and return to where they
  were: slow, late, not finished mending. Permanent loss is still reserved for Movement
  Five.
- **Team Seven.** Ninth of thirty-one by yield (72.0), **second step, attached**, to the
  Graywater Company under Rhea Sorn. Book: 2.0 sealed medical (W); 1.0 returned to the
  Wren House infirmary at par; a purse sheet and poles of its own (3.0); 2.0 for the six
  tries; 1.6 held at 0.4 a head. Anwen is away six weeks from the 10th; Kellan is weighing
  a Halden team; Perrin is named for his Hall's second team in the autumn.
- **Unsettled between people.** Anwen and Kiva (the gate, asked twice); Gower of Old Four;

- **Institutional.** The Circuit's book now reads "A gate shall be left lit" (from the
  night of the 30th). The Pit is shut until about the 13th. The Pit's tape opens to any
  chartered party on the **1st of the month after** (thirty days from Friday the 1st).
- **Knowledge.** Kiva holds four T-0 entries (2446, 2604, 2676, and the Pit's lamp ring,
  10:24 and about forty seconds) and the unread 2446 margin; she has kept them from the
  team and means to ask the Compact Library's reader herself. Noa knows of the lamps from
  the keeper and does not know what a T-0 is. Orla has named a bearer's book at the Home
  Line hall and asked Hollis Vane for it; Vane found an earlier hand in the field-direction
  ledger and cannot yet read or date it. Nobody connects the skin, the seventh reed, the
  class, the baselines, the T-0 entries and the disk.
- **Names and pronouns fixed in the repair.** Old Four's Kill is Gower and its Hold is
  Edda; the Kiln's warden is Sedge. Grader Halloran is she. Healer Aske is he (author's
  choice, pending owner review).
- **Lane calls.** "Half!" names the side that earned it; "Down!" names the side that lost
  a fighter.

  Dray's objection is still waiting on the Chair, with no date on the page.
- **The sequence ("a crossing").** Four sanctioned goes on the Circuit's travelling mast in
  the Old Yard: a called *off* (0.03), a crossing (0.30), a **stack** (0.11, called a miss by
  Noa from the tape), a crossing (0.31). **Two of Orla's six.** Spent 0.75 of the 2.00; **1.25
  in hand, which is four crossings and a hundredth.** Orla's six rules of the 12th stand (a
  step first; one go a night; the thread called cold aloud; *off* in the air if the Ember is
  not shut clean; the left hand shown first; Noa silent until after, and Perrin may stop it).
  A crossing costs about a quarter-dram in the gap with nothing open; a stack (the second
  door begun before the first is shut) costs a third as much, lands softly, and is forbidden.
- **The disk** stayed cold through both sanctioned crossings, the call, the reading and the
  hall. By the bearer's book it is "held of the Line. Released to a bearer," named by the
  bearer before and witnessed by a watch that does not choose; the Line records in print that
  it does not know what the plate is for and that its first book is lost. The watch of seven
  may recall it on a vote for cause written. Ilse Corran received it in 2643 from M. Gill and
  appointed Kiva in 2669; her written reason is a six-year-old's question ("Will they put
  why?"). The Line recorded that Mara said no and that it sealed the appointment regardless.
  **Kiva has entered the one warmth of the 29th in the fourth column in her own hand,**
  witnessed by Hollis Vane, who must read it to the watch on the 1st.
- **The dream.** No recurrence. Kiva has read Hesper Annick's fever entry (undated; the green
  notebook; the eleventh, disputed box) once, uncopied, under the 2611 settlement, with an
  observer from the Halden endowment and one from the Order of the Even Hand. It shares, with
  what Kiva had already told Mara, the roads, the unlike lights, the touching, the absent sky,
  the word "Full," the words "paying attention," every lamp answering, and some lamps going out
  and some white. It also has a color with no word for it, which Kiva has seen and never told
  anyone, so no page of hers can prove that match. It has no notes and no tick, and Annick
  stands among the lights where Kiva has only watched from a height. It gives no date,
  cause, meaning or use. Kiva has Annick's phrase, *the sky unroofed.* The sister saw her
  count from the third line to the last and had it recorded that the reader's lips moved.
  Mara did not read the entry; her page of the 29th is sealed and timed (1:52 p.m., the 25th)
  in the Library's press. The Order has written to Mara (six questions).
- **Body.** Knee passed for landings on the 13th, still strapped. The first two left fingers
  were white forty-one counts after the fourth go; the whole left hand went to the wrist at
  the sixth rung on the wharf and stayed away about fifteen hours, until Sunday supper, before
  coming back to its earlier state (Kiva corrected a note that said an hour). **Aske, the 26th:
  not burned; the wharf loss was the cord, four bag necks across the elbow, on a nerve the
  crossing had emptied;
  it "sulks a little longer" each time; no go until Monday; then one a week, with the hand
  seen the next morning.** Permanent loss is still reserved for Movement Five.
- **Certificate and conditions.** Unchanged. Kiva opened no door in the field. The stack is
  in Orla's weekly book and on a sanctioned tape; Ysra wants the tape, the reader and the
  sponsor on the 3rd.
- **Team Seven.** **Sixth of thirty-one by yield (116.8), still second step, attached**, to
  Rhea Sorn: it stood a whole Flask under a foreman with one remark (a lure lit inside a
  string's reading), which the shift officer took as the shift's. Book: 2.0 sealed medical
  (W), untouched; practice glass 1.25; 1.6 held; **8.4 credited (Grade First; W, full; I,
  trace) and not entered until four have signed.** The purse sheet is marked by a Flask's leg.
  Anwen is on Mace's summer line (culverts, then the Sag) and is to have the end of a wall;
  she and Kiva are unreconciled by Anwen's choice. Perrin calls *off* for Seven. **Kellan has
  accepted the Halden license team for the Regional** and stays on the book. Pru Finch is the
  standing reserve Hold on a yellow card ("a corner and a lamp. Not a wall."). No blade is
  named for the Regional.
- **The Weighbridge call.** Five husks and one skinned Flask from a tear that stopped keeping
  time. Tinned clean, 411.8 drams; one Handful printed at the gate and one lost down a grating
  by the shift officer's stated choice; the Pellow Building's core fouled; six of its cell
  boxes refilled before five from Kiva's second rung, on Warden Cobb's book. Hurt: Nairn
  (Graywater's second blade), left leg, both bones, until the autumn.
- **Stepped ash.** The comb's seventh reed is cut to Halden's foundation patent of 2391 (the
  Step), which claims "all ash exhibiting the Step, however produced" and cautions against
  introducing it "to an unstepped bank under load." Halden's yards and the big tram sheds are
  stepped; pump houses and tenement boxes are not. This spring's rift ash reads I, trace; the
  Flask's last two knots read **I, full** (113 drams, red-tagged, in the Wren House cold room).
  Three parties claim the bag: the Halden Works, the wardens of Weighbridge Street, and the
  Wren House reference shelf. Nobody has said why a tear throws it.
- **Instrument evidence.** The Pit's tape shows a notch on all sixteen pens eleven or twelve
  seconds after Kiva's cliff (this supersedes the "about forty seconds" in the Movement Three
  block above, which was the keeper's estimate), and nothing on the Low Lane mast two miles off. No notch
  followed either sanctioned crossing or anything at the Weighbridge. Unexplained.
- **Knowledge.** The 2446 margin is read (a sister of the Even Hand asked at the wicket
  before light whether the lamps had gone; the Warden ordered it left out). Anwen, Kellan,
  Perrin, Pru and Noa know of the four T-0 entries, the margin, the disputed box and Orla's
  2664 reading; none of them knows of the dream. Orla read the Annick entry in 2664 and will
  not tell Kiva what it says; Kiva will not tell Orla. Vane has seen the disk and the
  bearer's book; Orla has seen only Kiva's copied lines. Rhea Sorn told Kiva that Ilse Corran
  pulled her from a culvert with a hand shut on something at her throat, and that she will
  burn a card of two words; the words are not said. Mara does not yet know what the bearer's
  book records about her. Nobody connects the stepped ash, the T-0 entries, the notch, the
  disk and the dream.
- **Words.** "A crossing" is the Home Line's older word for a sequence (ledger entry of 2471,
  Tansy Wold). "A stack" is Kellan's and Orla's word for two doors open together. "Stepped"
  and "the Step" are Halden's; "counting ash" was the Wren House's earlier name.
- **Names and pronouns introduced (author's choice, pending owner review).** Lettice
  Arkwright ("Reader," she); Mr. Adlam (he); Pike, Nairn and Ruddock of Graywater (he);
  Warden Cobb (she); Dulcie (she) and Stott (he); Brannock (he); Tansy Wold (she); M. Gill
  (he); Pettit (a name only). Perrin's father and the sister of the Even Hand are unnamed.

## Movement Five carried state (chapters 33–40)

Recorded 2026-10-11 from the first draft, by its author, directly after the run.
**Unreviewed first draft.** The manuscript governs; full detail is in
`provenance/MOVEMENT-005-CONTINUITY.md`. Only what the prose shows is entered here.

- **Calendar.** The months are numbered on the page from this movement: the Pit was the fifth
  month, the Weighbridge the sixth; Movement Five runs from Sunday the 1st of the seventh to
  Monday the 26th of the ninth. Each still has thirty days. The watch sat on the 1st of the
  seventh; the Chair heard Selka Dray on the 2nd and all parties on the bag on the 3rd. The
  Regional Measure was Friday the 16th and Saturday the 17th of the ninth month.
- **The six.** Complete. Four more crossings at 0.31 each (the 2nd, 10th, 17th and 24th of the
  seventh month; the second of them on the Circuit's Great Floor), one a week, the hand seen
  the next morning. The practice glass ran 0.94, 0.63, 0.32, 0.01; the hundredth went into the
  team's next entry. Orla's certificate now adds: one crossing, Ember to still, low, to a
  mark, declared to a count, on a sanctioned mast read by the Chair's reader, one in seven
  days; not certified: any stack, any third door, anything she has not seen six times.
- **Three doors.** Stated on the page before any attempt: a competition (all open, fighting,
  nobody choosing), a stack (all open, agreeing; soft, cheap, and open to anything near), a
  sequence (each shut before the next opens, with two gaps). Kiva's order is hand, Ember,
  still ("catch, carry, plant"), priced at 0.59 whole. Five tries on a sanctioned mast in the
  eighth month: a jam (0.11), a hand-to-Ember crossing (0.30), an *off* (0.31), a stack at the
  second transition that she called herself (0.39), and **one clean (0.59, the 29th).** One
  more on the floor of the Pit on the 17th of the ninth month, under a recorded field
  direction, after the event was struck. **Not certified, not classified, and by the Chair's
  written entry "not to be asked of her."**
- **Body. The cost is paid.** The first two fingers of the left hand have **permanently lost
  fine sensation**: they bend, grip and report pressure; they do not tell edge from flat, hot
  from cold, or one thread from two (Healer Aske, the 24th of the ninth month). The rest of
  the hand returned within two days. Ward-trace ash and rest are both ruled out as remedies.
  It followed three choices shown on the page: declining the Order's year of rest; spending
  the week's one crossing in a lane on the 16th; and making two gaps the next day against a
  card that read *No go before the 23rd,* with the price read out first by her healer and her
  foreman. She catches with the first two fingers held clear ("passengers"), hands the cord to
  Pru Finch from the first turn, and needs help with a button.
- **The disk.** Cold throughout. The Home Line watch heard her entry; a recall for cause was
  moved and lost two to five. Written by the watch: the plate is held of the Line and borne by
  K. Rowan; it is not equipment, an instrument or evidence; no hand but the bearer's is laid
  on it without a vote; the bearer writes in the fourth column within the week after any
  crossing. One earlier fourth-column line in the night hand has been read: **Thirza Bly,
  bearer from 2466: "Warm, once, after the first crossing. I have crossed nine times since.
  Cold."** No cause, door or use is given. The School asked to borrow the plate and was
  answered from the fourth edition.
- **The dream.** No recurrence, and no fever on the night of the 17th. The Order of the Even
  Hand offered a year as a guest of its house, one door, every other shut; Kiva declined and
  Mara left the offer "on the latch."
- **Certificate and conditions.** Place, division and sponsor unchanged. Added by the Chair on
  the 2nd of the seventh month: no second door on any Circuit floor except a crossing declared
  to a count and read at the time from a sanctioned mast by the office's reader; a stack so
  read is an incident; not at the Bench; recovery only on any floor with a contained rift. The
  mast condition is now general, for any candidate with more than one door, under Selka
  Dray's name. No crossing counts unless the tape shows the cliff; Noa Bexley is reader to the
  office; Orla keeps her standing.
- **Stepped ash.** The Weighbridge bag: under three seals (the Works', the wardens', the
  Chair's) in the Wren House cold room; entered as made in Weighbridge Street; the street
  advanced 56.5 drams of plain stock by the casualty reserve; one dram each to the reference
  shelf and the Halden technical office; the patent claim entered and sent to the Compact's
  court, not ruled on. **New Circuit rules:** any catcher may red-tag a bag on stated grounds
  ("sufficient to withhold… not sufficient to prove"); no ash above I-trace on any Circuit
  floor; every Regional dram combed at the gate.
- **The Regional Measure.** Sixteen fours, four boards. Lane: Seven beat Old Four 8–7 and
  lost to Halden 8–6 (Kellan touched Kiva in the gap at the top of a declared crossing; a
  down; the crossing begun and not landed; not a stack; no incident). Bench: Seven third
  without Kiva. Carry: sixth. The Call was struck at 2:21 on the 17th and closed at 3:10 by
  the Home Line. **Result, read on three boards: 1. Halden License. 2. Old Four (att.). 3.
  Team Twenty-Eight. 4. T7 (att.).**
- **The hold.** During the Call four skinned husks, two of them unordered, stood a square
  over the Pit's race with a white cord run from each to the next and a tear inside it,
  outside the lattice. Lowen Brask, back from a summer on Home Line holds, named it. Selka
  Dray cut one corner inside her step on her catcher's word and was pinned by the cord (left
  leg above the knee, the bone, and a burn of cold). Kiva took the cord off her with the
  three-door sequence and held the corner ten counts; Perrin and Wilf Marten got Dray out;
  Kellan, Jude Talley and Gower cut the other three posts on Kiva's word as she released;
  Anwen called a four-ward student gate that took the new string; the closing crew closed
  the tear; Rhea Sorn and Halden's foreman cut two strings; each team bagged a rung. Nobody
  on the page says why spawn stood a hold. The Home Line has suspended held tears.
- **Instrument evidence.** At the Pit every lamp on the dead rim showed a point of light once
  (the keeper's second T-0 on that ring) and all sixteen pens notched eleven seconds after
  Kiva's second cliff; Mr. Adlam of the Halden technical office saw it and has written so
  under his own name. No notch followed the crossing on the Great Floor. Unexplained.
- **The grid.** The Ferren pump bank (twenty-four cells) went into step when the hold stood
  and **has not come out**, though its link was open; the pumps have not dropped a stroke.
  The Step is spreading along the Brickfields trunk at about a drum every two days (nineteen,
  twenty, twenty-one), with nine drums left before the Crooked Line School's core house.
  Nobody knows how to take a bank out of step.
- **Team Seven.** Fourth at the Regional; **still second step, attached** to Rhea Sorn, who
  has made no offer. Book: 2.0 sealed medical (W), never opened; **held 5.62; 18.0 credited
  from the Pit's pooled clean ash** (24.0 to each team; 6.0 by name to Jude Talley). Spent in
  this movement: 1.24 on four crossings, 2.1 on three Kennel floors, 1.0 returned at par to
  Weighbridge Street for the Pellow's boxes, 1.29 on three whole three-door tries (of a line
  of 1.8 that Perrin first refused and then proposed with his own brake). Seven's own rung
  from the Pit is red-tagged, stepped, on Kiva's tag. Jude stood the Regional as Kill by
  name; Anwen stood the Hold on her own terms; Kellan cut for Halden until the floor was
  struck and then declared for Seven under rule nineteen.
- **People.** Mara passed her re-sit on the 7th of the ninth month (Grade Two restored, the
  left her reading hand) and is reading the bank for Examiner Rusk. Anwen finished Mace's
  line without a dip and has a relief's place on the night van's ward line for next summer;
  she is "done minding" and has "not forgotten." Kellan has not answered Halden's offer of its
  yard for the winter. Perrin is passed to lift and tries the plate on the 29th. Orla wrote
  four lines to Jessamy Hart; no answer. Ysra Crest was a ward-holder on the 2660 Bench and
  had signed Hart's entry; she countersigned Orla's account of the hand, and has opened a page
  headed *Witnessed* with one entry. Selka Dray is unpersuaded and says so.
- **Words.** "A hold," "the hem" and "the corner" for the spawn square; "the fold" and
  "passengers" for the ward grip; "the Call"; "the Row"; "the Alderman"; "a step glass."
- **Names and pronouns introduced (author's choice, pending owner review).** Thirza Bly
  (she); Wilf Marten (he); Hesketh (she), Yarrow (he) and Foreman Birch (he) of the Halden
  license four, with Pettit (he) now on the page; Wace (he) of Old Four; Tibb and Stiles (he)
  of Graywater's ward line. The watch officers go by their watches (the Ferren is she).
